### B3 Execution Ledger — 2026-11-17

- **Authorization/scope:** owner explicitly authorized B3 after B2. C0 review/freeze remains at M1 closure, not waived or auto-satisfied. Existing dirty B1/B2 work was preserved on `feature_mannan`; no staging, commit, reset or branch switch. No M2–M7 output implementation, Express gateway, frontend, frozen P1–P4 domain/contract, ADR, dependency, CI or master changes.
- **Fixed dependencies/report barrier:** extended P5 repository/coordinator and guarded publication UoW. Under bundle → sorted current work locks, a completion receipt participates only as this transaction's tentative completion (subject to the final lease/fence CAS), or as a persisted COMPLETED attempt/work/receipt with exact identity/fence. Validate exact producer READY references through guarded transaction-enlisted public ports; check each diagram scope, profile, semantic input and canonical digest. Four distinct pinned templates create one report current slot and BarrierReceipt atomically with the fourth completion; report input is the tagged barrier fingerprint. First three commits, arbitrary/mixed refs, invalid readiness and rolled-back publication create no report. No broker send or producer Session callback announces acceptance before commit.
- **Overview and recovery:** overview committed READY releases siblings; durable terminal FAILED releases them with monotonic `DependencyReceipt(reason=overview_final_failure)` including failed work identity/policy/time. RETRYING and CANCELLED do not release them. Reconciliation recovers lost hints and missing dispatch independently. An authorized same-input overview successor can complete the first four-ref set while reusing valid siblings. Existing report pins never change; manual report successors retain/revalidate the same exact four references. External producer readiness failures fail closed, not a latest-pointer substitution or synthetic artifact.
- **C0 candidate refinements:** `WorkExecutionContext.prerequisites` carries the immutable `BarrierReceipt` for report consumers; the coordinator requires it and validates the input fingerprint before executing a report. Non-report executions carry None. Readiness is revalidated before execution and inside accepted publication. `PublicationUnitOfWork.coordinate` is an M1-only guarded dependency transaction, never a producer lifecycle API. C0 remains an unfrozen candidate at the agreed version pending final owner/producer-consumer review; no frozen upstream contracts changed.
- **Retry operations:** strict `{resource_type,resource_id,idempotency_key}` uses the failed **work/run UUID** as resource_id, not an external artifact/generation ID. OWNER/ADMIN only; scope/type/current FAILED eligibility, classified transient exhaustion, identical pinned input/profile, registered handler, compatible fresh fleet, dispatch/execution approval, dependencies, manual round cap and project/actor admission windows are enforced. Project admission lock → bundle/run lock serializes competing retries; stable actor/project/action/key + body fingerprint returns the original accepted response even after completion, conflicting reuse/different keys racing the same predecessor cannot create a second successor. Terminal predecessors/attempts remain unchanged. No public cancel endpoint. Unknown handler exceptions are now nonretryable by default; only typed transient failures and explicit execution-stop/ownership-loss paths may retry. Terminal transient exhaustion has a durable `safe_reason=retryable_exhausted`, while the attempt retains its actual failure code; cancellation JSON is not reused for failure metadata.
- **Operational policy:** added explicit, default-free `ManualRetryPolicy(max_rounds,window_seconds,max_actor_requests,max_project_requests)` and blank `DELIVERY_MANUAL_RETRY_JSON` in `.env.example` and ignored `.env`. Its absence yields a clear retry-policy conflict, never unlimited restarts. Test policy values are not production defaults. Dispatch-off rollout/drain may finish approved existing work but cannot accept new retry intent. Existing production dispatch/operational approval remain false; no application migrations were applied and no additional schema/migration was needed for B3.
- **API/composition:** new `api_contracts.py` and `backend/api/v1/delivery.py`, additive include in `backend/api/v1/router.py`. GET `/api/v1/architecture-versions/{version_id}/delivery?project_id=...` and POST `.../delivery/retry?project_id=...`; delegated service authentication/project context reused, all four recognized roles read, only OWNER/ADMIN retry (separate from P3's broader operator permission). Exact public ArchitectureVersion DTO + P4 source + analysis-read port establish requested identity/eligibility, never latest. Accepted scopes poll without reloading canonical payloads: a bounded configured-policy lookup compares immutable stored scope against exact public published-version metadata; mismatches fail closed. Status is nonmutating and explains NOT_SCHEDULED/runtime/handler/worker/reconciler/dependency waits, safe counters, retained accepted refs and cancellation cause; no raw lease secret, lease digest, dispatch epoch or source bytes. Local errors preserve `{error:{code,message,details}}` with 404/409/503 and documented auth statuses; existing FastAPI 422/auth handlers remain untouched. Invalid runtime configuration returns unavailable without exposing configuration values. Python-only APIs await separately owned M6 transport. No Express endpoint is claimed available.

## 7. Detailed P5 M2 Plan — Knowledge Projection and Search Index

### 7.1 Status, authority and repository findings

**OWNER APPROVED FOR IMPLEMENTATION — B1/B2/B3 NOT STARTED.** The owner approved the refined M2 plan and explicitly instructed not to begin implementation yet. Exactly three batches and their technical/approval gates are retained as the agreed baseline. Plan approval is not real-service validation, C1 freeze, approval of unmeasured numerical values/an untested ES pair, or separate authorization to modify frozen M1. Earlier proposal/not-authorized wording records the pre-approval planning state and is superseded for overall M2 plan status only. Only this document was edited during planning/status recording on `feature_mannan`; pre-existing B1–B3 worktree changes were preserved. No tests, migration, service setup or dependency installation was performed for this plan.

Authority: master §§12.58, 13.24–28, 13.49, 13.56, 13.69, 14.8–14, 18.20/21/53; accepted ADR-0001; approved P5 baseline §§7, 15.2, 19–25; frozen C0 §6/10.2 above. Master names conceptual document categories, not mandatory new canonical types. The phase baseline's draft header is historical. This detailed proposal refines its file/component sketches without pretending missing interfaces already exist.

| Verified existing path | What exists / reuse / limitation |
|---|---|
| `backend/platform/analysis/architecture/model.py` | Immutable ArchitectureDocument: entities, relationships, evidence, unresolved_relationships, conflicts, completeness, source_diagnostic_ids; content digest and source versions. Entities carry logical IDs, not persistence UUID fields. No diagnostic message collection or repository document bodies is present |

These are dependencies, not sketches to reimplement |

No public completed-work successor/reprojection method was found |

Python 3.14.6 observed; importlib confirms elasticsearch is not installed. No ES endpoint/installed version or live compatibility was verified |

Real-service tests require explicit isolated DB URL with current stacksense_p5_b2_ prefix; do not silently retarget application DB or broaden that fixture. Add M2-owned fixtures and schemas instead |

No `backend/platform/knowledge/` implementation, ES mappings, search API, embedding provider, generation store or sealed ES proof exists. No applicable AGENTS.md was found in the repository search. Tests passing for M1 is not evidence that any of these exist.

### 7.2 Ownership, deliverables and non-goals

M2 owns deterministic KnowledgeProjection/C1, ES mappings and bounded indexing/readback, immutable generation descriptors, producer-local accepted resource/pointer metadata, exact-generation lexical search primitives, search freshness and reader/PIT/orphan safety. Search stays independent of diagrams/report and Neo4j. M2 must not turn search resource metadata into a second execution engine.

- M1 alone discovers/qualifies publications, creates/claims work, controls attempt lifecycle/fence/epoch, dispatches/heartbeats/retries/recovers and commits the outer publication transaction. M2 only implements its search producer and scoped read ports.
- M2 constructs/writes/verifies/seals a candidate outside M1's publication transaction. Inside its borrowed guarded Session M2 stages its own READY descriptor/pointer and AcceptedOutput. M1's final fence CAS and commit atomically accept these with its receipt/completion. M2 never commits, changes M1 rows or activates ES aliases.
- M3 owns retrieval planning, ambiguity resolution, graph-assisted queries, ContextBundle, evidence expansion and optional excerpt authorization. M2 supplies bounded lexical methods and provenance, not natural-language intent, graph traversal, source reads or AI.
- M6 later owns user-facing transport and React integration, with delegated authentication/RBAC. No Express, frontend, M3 context, diagram/report/artifact-file implementation is included here. M2-owned FastAPI search/status routes may be added at B3; no direct browser access or new JWT mechanism.
- Semantic scope reconciliation: master §2.17 and §14.6 retain textual AND semantic search as overall product responsibilities; §13.24 specifies semantic/vector retrieval where configured. Approved P5 baseline §7.3 and D4 establish mandatory lexical + existing graph-assisted retrieval (the graph-assisted composition belongs to M3) and capability-gated semantic/hybrid retrieval, with initial semantic acceptance explicitly left to owner decision. Thus neither “mandatory semantic in this M2” nor “approved permanent semantic deferral” is established by the repository. Recommend explicitly approving lexical-only initial P5 delivery with semantic/hybrid deferred pending provider approval; this recommendation is NOT itself the scope decision. If owner requires semantics in initial P5, provider/model/profile and semantic integration are a hard acceptance blocker; do not mark the phase complete with disabled capability. Lexical supplies exact identifiers/names, keyword/text ranking and scoped filters; semantic would add approved embedding-based similarity and hybrid rank fusion, not architecture inference. No installed provider is evidence of implementation absence, not authority to reduce scope.

### 7.3 Complete data flow and recovery

1. Frozen M1 discovers an exact published successful analysis/version and creates its independent search_projection/no-template work with pinned ProducerProfile, execution policy and input fingerprint. M2 installs no competing publication scanner/task/queue.
2. M1 resolves the exact registered profile and supplies WorkExecutionContext. M2 validates scope, slot, profile and schema compatibility; checkpoints before/after bounded work. It cannot refresh leases or alter fences. Load the exact canonical document through injected PublishedArchitectureSource, compare all source identities/versions/configuration/content digest with VersionScope and reject missing/mismatched input.
3. Pure KnowledgeProjector generates deterministic safe records and expected key/count/payload digests. Validate canonical references before storage. Exceeding an approved count/byte/resource bound fails with a stable capacity code, never silent truncation. Build bounded record batches rather than repeatedly materializing full JSON copies.
4. Persist M2-owned candidate metadata bound to the entire AttemptIdentity, a fresh generation UUID and immutable artifact UUID. Record its pinned physical index identity/schema/settings hash. This is unaccepted staging metadata, never READY. No candidate adoption by another attempt, even with identical bytes.
5. SearchIndexWriter uses bounded create-only bulk requests, inspects every item and waits for completion. Duplicate create is accepted only after exact scope/key/payload equality; overwrite/update/upsert are forbidden. All uncertain writes, automatic SDK/helper retries and delayed in-flight requests must be accounted for before verification/sealing. Bounded transport retry cannot invent a new M1 attempt/budget.
6. Follow CREATE → INDEX → VERIFY → DRAIN/SEAL → INDEPENDENT READBACK → PREPARE → M1 ACCEPTANCE. Initial VERIFY checks bounded item acknowledgements and expected candidate inventory before sealing; it is not the final completeness proof. DRAIN/SEAL establishes physical write exclusion; refresh afterward and independently scan the exact immutable candidate. SearchVerifier compares expected versus actual keys/counts/payload/digests and scope/schema/profile/index UUID. No write acknowledgement/count-only proof. Unknown write completion, drain, shard acknowledgement or seal state fails closed and leaves an unpublished orphan. Section 7.5 defines the proposed seal boundary and proof requirements.
7. Durably store the immutable verification/seal descriptor and primary manifest bytes outside M1 UoW. Return PreparedOutput(attempt=context.attempt, candidate=CandidateSeal(...)). M1 checkpoints and opens its publication UoW; producer's enlisted factory validates bounded local sealed metadata and exact PreparedOutput/PublicationContext, stages READY/reference and pointer, returns AcceptedOutput/ReadyValidation. No external ES calls/full scans/alias swap occur in this transaction. M1 alone can accept/commit.
8. Authorized readers first resolve a committed exact ArtifactReference/resource pointer in PostgreSQL, acquire a bounded generation read pin, then query the pinned physical index UUID/generation. Verify response scope and terminal request state, finish mapping and recheck the active pointer before returning active-generation search. Historical explicit-reference selection was a planned extension; the final selected-direction C1 candidate defers it pending formal confirmation, rather than claiming it implemented. Never search an unaccepted candidate, fallback index/alias or different version.
9. Crash before acceptance leaves an orphan; M1 replays/retries through a NEW attempt/candidate under its existing rules. Ambiguous SQL commit is resolved through committed M1 receipt/producer metadata, not rewriting the sealed candidate. M2 does not reopen attempts, reuse another attempt's candidate or tell M1 to recompute upstream analysis.
10. Failed/retrying work uses frozen M1 failure/retry interfaces. A successful/completed-generation rebuild, lost accepted ES data or mapping/profile upgrade needs an authorized new logical run; **the current frozen M1 public surface does not provide that operation**. Section 7.12 identifies the exact blocked integration decision. No private M1 insert/reset, separate M2 worker or fake 202 is permitted.

### 7.4 Proposed C1 v1.0.0 vocabulary, records and deterministic encoding

**Proposed C1, not frozen.** Use strict, deep-immutable typed DTOs and reject unsupported version/unknown fields; import C0 VersionScope/ArtifactReference/ProducerProfile rather than redefine them. C1 schema, projector and mapping/analyzer configuration fingerprints are explicit and pinned by the exact producer configuration fingerprint. C0 input_fingerprint remains the M1-supplied opaque value; do not replace it with a projection digest.

| Record kind | Source, searchable fields and preservation rules |
|---|---|
| entity | One per canonical logical_id; name/title, exact kind/namespace/status, evidence refs, selected safe architectural metadata. Confirmed/supported/inferred/unresolved/conflicting remain static source statuses |
| relationship | One per canonical logical_id; actual source/target logical IDs and direction, relationship kind/status/evidence refs. Title is a deterministic static label, not runtime narrative. Preserve containment versus dependency distinction |
| evidence | One per canonical evidence logical_id; source_record_type, safe repository-relative path, optional artifact UUID/fact key/range/producer/version, original strength/polarity. Do not invent absent source_fact_key, normalize coordinates anew or index snippets/secret values |
| unresolved | One per existing unresolved logical_id; source, bounded safe target descriptor, original reason/status, candidate/evidence references. It is uncertainty, not a resolved architecture edge/entity |
| conflict | One per existing conflict logical_id; conflict_type, subject_key, evidence references; competing evidence retained. No inferred resolution or free-form details dump |
| architecture_summary | One deterministic summary for the exact version: source completeness/counts and source_diagnostic_ids as references only. No generated prose, inferred severity, diagnostic messages or new findings |

Documentation/configuration evidence uses existing evidence records only where the canonical source_record_type/strength establishes it. No raw README/config ingestion, second source-analysis pass, diagnostic-table query or synthetic missing-evidence record. Every referenced entity/evidence must exist within the exact source or be a typed documented external reference; dangling canonical edges fail candidate verification. Invalid required unsafe fields fail safely, never disappear. Unapproved optional metadata is excluded by explicit schema with omission counts/field names (not values).

Proposed common KnowledgeRecord fields: c1_version, scope (same C0 VersionScope), generation_id, document_kind, canonical_reference, knowledge_key, storage_key, title, text_fields (closed named fields), kind/status where applicable, source_logical_id/target_logical_id where applicable, evidence_ids, source_reference, safe_metadata, record_digest. source_reference preserves actual logical/artifact/range/provenance fields; no inferred UUID identity. Persistence entity/relationship UUIDs are optional until obtained through exported public DTOs; logical references are always exact. No invented ID formula or frozen persistence-repository import. Internal ES envelope additionally binds producer profile, mapping/projector versions, canonical digest and physical generation ownership. Physical index name/UUID remain server-only metadata, not public hit fields.

Metadata policy: closed per-kind schemas, not arbitrary metadata/details copying or name-based redaction. Initial proposed safe fields: framework, architectural_role, runtime_role, application_namespace, method, path (route-relative only), database_engine, infrastructure_kind, dependency_name, manifest_declared and source_referenced with field-specific validation. No connection strings, URLs with host/userinfo/query, literal secret values, unrestricted target_descriptor/conflict details, host paths, source body or environment content. Repository-relative source paths reject traversal/absolute/control characters and are never opened by M2. Unsupported metadata omission is visible; oversized valid required content fails rather than truncates. Adversarial canonical labels are untrusted; validate schema/bytes and escape only at downstream render time.

Encoding proposal: J(value) = UTF-8 JSON, sorted object keys, compact separators, ensure_ascii=false, finite numbers only; UUIDs lowercase standard strings, enums exact values, set-like arrays sorted by explicit stable key, source-ordered arrays preserved. Preserve canonical logical IDs and Unicode code points exactly, no NFC/casefold for identity; reject invalid Unicode/surrogates. Search-only analysis may lowercase/tokenize text without modifying identity. Include c1_version in the typed canonical input; do not use frozen P4's NUL encoding or C0's tagged fingerprint helper as if interchangeable with these hashes.

- knowledge_key = SHA256(J({c1_version, identity:[project_id, architecture_version_id, document_kind, canonical_reference]})); summary canonical_reference is a reserved typed summary marker, separate from all logical-ID record kinds.
- storage_key = SHA256(J({c1_version, identity:[knowledge_key, generation_id]})); use as ES _id and store it for verification/sorting. Same semantic version/record retains knowledge_key across retries; new generations have different storage keys.
- record semantic digest covers all allowlisted semantic payload fields and exact scope's semantic_identity; excludes publication timestamp, generation/storage identity, verification timestamps and ES engine metadata. A stored-record digest additionally covers the entire exact expected ES _source envelope, excluding its own digest field. Neither depends on _score.
- projection digest hashes the ordered tuples (knowledge_key, semantic record digest) plus source canonical digest/profile/schema/mapping fingerprints. Candidate digest hashes the ordered tuples (storage_key, stored-record digest) plus generation/index UUID/version scope. Define domain tags inside each C1 hash input. Digests exclude their own fields. Test golden UTF-8 byte vectors for every domain.
- SearchManifest is canonical immutable primary artifact bytes containing scope/profile/input fingerprint, candidate/generation/artifact IDs, exact index identity/settings/schema, expected per-kind counts and key-set/payload digests, verifier/seal protocol versions and proof reference. Content digest/size in C0 describe exactly these manifest bytes, not summed document bytes or an ES response. Observation timestamps go in a separately persisted audit record, not semantic fingerprints. No spec/layout digests for search.

### 7.5 ES mappings, physical sealing and independent verification

The approved baseline is versioned shared physical indexes per schema. **Proposed scoped amendment, NOT APPROVED:** one physical index per immutable generation, selected for stronger independently demonstrable exclusion, conditional on acceptable measured overhead. Mandatory project/version/generation filters, copied canonical identities and C1 keys remain unchanged under either layout. This is not an amendment to master, C0 or P1–P4. Until owner approval the shared baseline remains authoritative; no implementer may switch layouts implicitly.

Proposed strict mapping: UUIDs/digests/keys/kinds/status/provenance IDs are keyword; counts/range integers are bounded numeric; title and explicitly named text fields use a pinned lexical analyzer plus exact keyword field where safe; references are keyword arrays, no dynamic arbitrary objects; raw source/secret bodies and embedding vectors absent. Disable automatic index creation and dynamic mapping; validate exact index UUID/mapping/settings at open, write, verify and read. A recreated same-name index is a different physical identity and fails reference checks. Provisioning, candidate writing, reading, sealing and cleanup are distinct least-privilege roles, trusted fixed endpoint, TLS verification and explicit secret handling. No superuser in query/worker logs.

**Sealing is an implementation approval gate, not solved by sealed=true or op_type=create.** Elastic's create_doc privilege prevents overwrite, not later additions; its write block applies to an entire index, not an individual generation inside a shared index. Application row state/advisory locking alone is not an ES-level seal against already authorized in-flight writes. The approved C0 requires actual storage write exclusion before PreparedOutput. B1 must demonstrate a complete protocol, including unknown bulk outcomes, replica/refresh visibility and cleanup races, before B2 can claim READY.

| Criterion | A — shared physical index | B — generation-specific physical index, recommended subject to proof/cost approval |
|---|---|---|
| In-flight requests and late retries | Index-wide blocking also stops unrelated generations. Credential invalidation alone does not prove all authorized in-flight requests drained; no supported generation-level barrier has been demonstrated | Dedicated add-block/write operation supplies an index-wide barrier; require exact selected-version drain semantics and full shard acknowledgements, plus adversarial late-write tests, not a settings PUT alone |
| Post-seal worker power | create_doc still permits extra documents; shared write permission cannot be removed for one generation by a row flag | Candidate-specific create_doc role has no index manage/settings/delete/auto-create privilege; sealed index rejects further writes even with the old candidate credential. Provision/seal credentials never enter WorkExecutionContext/worker handler |
| Identity, completeness and reads | Independent identification of a mis-tagged generation document requires stronger ownership proof than filtered counts | Match-all readback of exact concrete index independently checks every document, including wrongly tagged documents; verify recorded UUID before/after readback and reads. No alias/wildcard target |
| Cleanup | Must delete only proven candidate documents while preserving others; sealed shared index cannot permit selective writes without breaking its seal | Delete exact unaccepted index only after retirement/reference/pin/grace/exclusion checks; never clear the write block for cleanup. Name/UUID revalidation and exclusive generation ownership required |
| Operating cost | Amortized shards/segments/cluster metadata; more complex immutable isolation/security proof | More indices/shards, empty-segment overhead, heap, file handles, startup/recovery/cluster-state latency and retained-history disk; requires measured admission ceilings, not unlimited per-generation creation |
| Multi-project/retention | Efficient pooled capacity, but unrelated writers share sealing blast radius | Independent build/seal/failure domains; aggregate resource admission must account for all projects, retained versions, retries, old active + new candidate and orphan backlog |

No complete shared-index generation exclusion protocol is verified today. A row flag, advisory lock, create-only ID or task cancellation alone is insufficient. Do not invent an untested third layout as an escape hatch. If B's resource cost fails approved capacity requirements and A cannot prove physical sealing, record M2 BLOCKED rather than weaken C0 or silently truncate retention. Actual ES APIs/privilege behavior must be tested on the selected supported pair; documentation availability is not deployment proof.

**Recommended B seal protocol (proposal):**

1. CREATE a uniquely named exact-generation concrete index through restricted provisioning with pinned mappings/settings; persist its UUID/ownership before writer access. Disable auto-create and never reuse this name. Worker gets only candidate-scoped create access; reader/verification, sealing and deletion credentials remain separate trusted M2 infrastructure capabilities, not M1 task data.
2. INDEX bounded create-only batches with explicit finite retry policy and checkpoints. Track every submission/completion and stop scheduling new work before the seal barrier. No fire-and-forget helper/background futures. Unknown bulk outcomes cannot be treated as success.
3. VERIFY local acknowledgements/expected inventory and reject known errors; this provisional check cannot authorize publication.
4. DRAIN/SEAL: quiesce the writer, await tracked requests within budget, then call the selected version's dedicated exact-index add-block/write API. Require acknowledged=true, shards_acknowledged=true and the exact index's blocked=true, with UUID/mapping revalidation. B1 must establish that successful shard barrier completion drains previously admitted writes; if not guaranteed, no candidate may proceed. Late arriving requests must be rejected by the block. Timeout/partial ack/uncertain identity fails closed; never remove a possible block or continue writes to repair a candidate.
5. **Immutability boundary:** confirmed physical barrier plus all tracked submissions resolved, exact UUID and restricted worker unable to unblock/delete/recreate. Block metadata alone, before drain acknowledgement, is not this boundary. The sealing role is trusted narrowly scoped infrastructure, not a malicious-cluster-admin sandbox; audit administrative unblock/index deletion as integrity incidents. M1 publication receives only proof metadata, never those credentials.
6. INDEPENDENT READBACK: refresh the sealed index, independently enumerate match-all complete inventory and validate §7.5 keys/fields/digests/scopes; reject shard failures/partial search/late extras. Recheck UUID, mappings and write block after scan. Seal proof records API/version, exact UUID/settings, acknowledgement outcome and verification digest; timestamps are audit, not semantic hashes. A sealed but invalid candidate is abandoned, not unblocked/patched.
7. PREPARE: persist immutable primary manifest and durable sealed/readback descriptor, then return C0 PreparedOutput. M1 ACCEPTANCE enlists local metadata only and commits the accepted reference atomically with final fencing. Visibility begins at that commit, not physical seal. Exact-index reads stay stable; corruption/unblock/loss fails explicitly, never substitutes another generation.

Real proof must include a bulk crossing the barrier, delayed retry after barrier, old/stale worker credentials, attempted new create/update/delete/unblock/recreation, process/ES restart, partial shard ack, response loss, UUID replacement and concurrent unrelated project writes. Measure cost for empty/small/large generations, expected retained history and repeated failures; total shards = sum over indices of primary shards × (1 + replica count), with actual bytes/heap/file descriptors/cluster-state and recovery latency reported. Test shard/replica values are experiments, not approved production values. Admission blocks new builds before exhaustion while preserving accepted history; no automatic history deletion to make the preferred layout look cheap. B2 is blocked until owner approves layout/protocol AND the real-service proof and measured resource envelope.

Independent verifier algorithm: recompute expected inventory from the immutable KnowledgeProjection, not writer success counters. Establish refresh/seal visibility, open bounded PIT/readback, require all shards complete/no timed_out/partial results; enumerate all documents in the exact candidate namespace, validating full stored fields and recomputing digest rather than trusting stored hashes. Verify exact key sets including extras/missing/duplicates; _id=storage_key; logical-kind key uniqueness; all scope/profile/schema/mapping/index UUIDs; original relationship direction/endpoints and evidence references; per-kind/total counts and ordered aggregate digest. Missing canonical documents, unindexed evidence, unknown extra fields, tampering or substituted same-name index fails. Use pagination with bounded memory and deadline/checkpoints; close PIT in finally. Empty models have an explicit summary/count manifest, never vacuous count-only success. Capped tests include a match-all candidate inventory under least-privilege administrative verification so incorrect scope tags cannot be hidden by ordinary query filters; if shared-index ownership cannot independently identify mis-tagged/orphan writes, verification must fail, not declare complete.

### 7.6 PostgreSQL ownership, publication and cleanup

Proposed M2-owned tables under shared Base: SearchResource (unique project/version/profile, stable resource UUID and accepted generation pointer/revision), SearchGeneration (full AttemptIdentity JSON plus indexed scope/work/attempt/candidate IDs; generation/artifact UUIDs; sealed manifest bytes/digest/size; physical index identity; verification/seal metadata; acceptance/cleanup state), SearchReadPin (bounded owner/deadline/exact generation and PIT association). Add unique exact ownership keys, scope-composite foreign keys and accepted pointer integrity, byte/size checks and indexes for bounded exact lookups/staging cleanup. One M2 migration creates only these tables; revision ID allocated from actual Alembic head during implementation, never guessed here. **No new SearchAttempt/lease/fence/retry counter table.** Original phase-level search-attempt sketch is satisfied by M1 attempts referenced as opaque immutable IDs, not duplicate lifecycle.

Producer-local candidate states are STAGING → SEALED → ACCEPTED, or STAGING/SEALED → DELETING → DELETED. These are storage metadata, not a public delivery lifecycle. Immutable accepted descriptors cannot be overwritten or tombstoned automatically. Verification failure is persisted as a safe M2 observation; M1 records execution failure. New staging bookkeeping uses M2-owned short transactions; it cannot claim acceptance. Enlisted SearchPublicationPort locks resource then generation consistently, rejects unsealed/deleting/wrong-owner references, verifies exact local manifest/digest/profile/verification proof, inserts immutable accepted metadata and updates resource pointer. It returns AcceptedOutput with exactly the C0 candidate fields and stable M2 resource_id. M1 owns final fence validation and physical commit. Historical OutputReadinessReader validates the original accepted generation using context.require_reference, even after the active pointer changes; no live historical lease or external call is required.

Cleanup is producer-owned but never inferred from elapsed M1 lease: obtain AttemptRetirementReader result before producer locks; unknown/live/DB failure stops. Serialize candidate/pointer/pins versus publication under the same M2 metadata lock order; recheck accepted/historical/report references, active readers/PITs, unresolved external operations and approved grace. Durably mark deletion, release transaction, delete only the exact unaccepted namespace using bounded verified IDs/index UUID, then persist completion; recover idempotently. A late publisher rejects DELETING. Never hold M1 UoW or producer row locks across ES deletion. Shared indexes require exact-generation delete batches, never wildcard/delete whole shared index; generation indexes require exact UUID + exclusive ownership before index deletion. No published/historical deletion without separate retention approval. Missing grace/limits disables destructive cleanup, not unlimited/default immediate deletion.

Reader pin acquisition and cleanup marking serialize on generation metadata; lease expiry alone does not permit deletion while an ES request/PIT may still exist. Close/cancel/confirm bounded requests and expire PIT before reclaim, or fail closed. PIT renewal cannot exceed approved pin/session bounds. No permanent background janitor queue is introduced: bounded M2 maintenance is exposed as a public producer service; wiring into existing maintenance requires explicit composition scope, not changes to frozen M1 recovery semantics.

### 7.7 Freshness and exact-version read semantics

SearchStatus is an orthogonal projection of committed M2 metadata, frozen M1 WorkSummary and bounded dependency health, not a second source of attempt state. Proposed fields: scope, resource_state, freshness, active_reference?, attempt_state?, is_readable, is_current_version (true/false/unknown), last_verified_at?, last_failure_code?, capabilities, unavailable_reason?. Resource state vocabulary remains PENDING/RUNNING/COMPLETED/FAILED/STALE; M1's QUEUED/RETRYING appear only in attempt/work dimensions.

| Condition | Freshness / visibility |
|---|---|
| Exact search work is pending/queued/running/retrying, no readable generation | BUILDING; resource PENDING/RUNNING; is_readable=false |
| Verified accepted active generation matches exact current version and pinned profile | CURRENT; COMPLETED; readable when store healthy |
| Accepted exact historical version, or accepted older profile retained pending approved replacement | STALE; STALE; readable, never substituted for the requested newer version |
| Candidate rebuild active while older accepted generation exists | BUILDING plus explicit readable active_reference; resource retains accepted COMPLETED/STALE, work dimension shows progress |
| Terminal failed work, no accepted generation | FAILED; FAILED; false; bounded safe failure code |
| Failed rebuild with accepted generation | Retain CURRENT or STALE according to exact version/profile; last_failure_code and work FAILED show failure. Historical STALE stays STALE/readable |
| ES missing/unhealthy/corrupt reference, incompatible mapping or disabled capability | UNAVAILABLE with explicit reason; retain committed pointer/history, do not rewrite M1 success or manufacture FAILED work |

UNAVAILABLE takes display precedence when physical reads cannot be served; readiness metadata is not proof of present store health. Existing accepted generation can remain readable with an unavailable new handler. Newer model V2 with unbuilt search never returns V1 hits as V2. Repository-level UI may explicitly show a separately labeled V1 stale result only after selecting its exact scope.

Current-version computation is not provided by get_for_analysis. M2-owned read adapter may sweep the existing public published-header port under a bounded budget and maintain an observed newest repository sequence/version with observed_at; unknown/incomplete observation must return is_current_version=null rather than certify CURRENT. Authoritative exact-route identity never depends on that hint. C1 approval must choose freshness-current semantics (latest observed published version with disclosed timestamp recommended) and cache TTL; no direct P3 table query or invented latest endpoint. Concurrency tests include multiple versions for the same analysis, earlier-sort insertions and publication-before-eligibility.

### 7.8 C1 internal interfaces and API boundary

Proposed public interfaces (new, not existing): KnowledgeProjector.project(document, scope, profile); SearchIndexWriter.prepare(projection, context); SearchVerifier.verify(expected, physical_candidate, control, deadline); SearchWorkHandler.execute(context) → C0 PreparedOutput; SearchPublicationFactory(GuardedEnlistedSession) → SearchPublicationPort; SearchGenerationReader.resolve_exact(scope, reference?) → accepted generation descriptor/read pin; SearchProjectionService.status(scope, work_summary); SearchReader.search(request, authorization_scope, deadline) → SearchPage; SearchMaintenance.cleanup(...). M2 readiness implements existing validate_ready(reference, PublicationContext) → ReadyValidation. Methods receive public types/DI, not M1 repositories or lease tokens. Constructors take explicit validated operational profiles, not magic defaults.

SearchRequest proposal: exact project/version scope, q, mode=lexical, repeated document_kind/entity_kind/status allowlisted filters, limit, cursor?. Query-length/bytes/filter/page/deadline limits remain measurement/approval gates. No raw DSL, query_string operators, wildcard regex, arbitrary index, script, sorts, field names or external URL. Literal bounded multi_match plus explicit exact-ID/name matches; exact identifier tier precedes text tier, stable knowledge_key tie-break. ES BM25 scores are presentation/ranking values, not evidence strength or deterministic cross-generation truth. C1 pins fields/boosts/analyzer/rank tiers and relevance fixtures; exact ranking reproducibility is within the same immutable index/PIT/profile, not all shard layouts.

SearchHit proposal: knowledge_key, document_kind, canonical_reference, title, canonical kind/status if applicable, bounded safe source/evidence references, rank tier/score and exact scope; no storage locators or source fragments. SearchPage: c1_version, scope, generation_id, items, next_cursor?, has_more, bounded completeness/omission reasons and capabilities. SearchVerificationResult: full scope/attempt/candidate/generation/profile/index UUID, expected/actual per-kind counts, ordered key/payload digests, mapping/seal/verifier versions, success/failure codes. Internal only; immutable result does not itself prove SQL acceptance. ResolvedSearchGeneration contains the committed ArtifactReference plus server-only exact index identity, never a client-selected physical target.

Pagination: signed opaque HMAC cursor binds actor authorization scope/project/version, generation, physical index UUID, query/filter/rank/profile digests, canonical sort/search_after, PIT handle/key reference, expiry, cursor/key version and read-pin identity. Initial scope authorization occurs on every call before ES. Use fixed literal queries and mandatory scope filters before retrieval/ranking, validate every hit afterward. Use PIT/session-stable search_after, latest returned PIT ID managed server-side. A changed active generation or query/scope rejects continuation with 409; expired cursor/PIT 410; bad signature/shape 422; no silent restart. Close/exhaust pins on final page/error. Do not expose index credentials or raw SDK bodies via cursors. Historical explicit-reference pagination is deferred in the final selected-direction candidate pending formal owner confirmation; exact historical Architecture Version reads are retained.

One whole-operation monotonic deadline includes SQL resolution/pin, ES execution and iteration, mapping, final SQL pointer check and cleanup. Disable hidden unbounded client retries, reject partial shards/timed_out responses. A transport timeout is not proof ES work stopped; B1 must verify selected client's cancellable task/PIT behavior and server timeout/cancellation limits. If hard cancellation cannot be guaranteed, bound remaining server work and retain its pin until completion/expiry; no unsafe cleanup or claim of confirmed termination. No enlargement/redefinition of frozen P4 query deadlines.

Planned Python routes use V=`/api/v1/architecture-versions/{version_id}` and existing trusted delegated context/project checks:

| Endpoint | Proposed contract / owner |
|---|---|
| GET V/search/status?project_id=... | 200 SearchStatus, including explicit unavailable/building state; unknown/foreign exact published version 404; invalid delegation 401/403. M2 status, no writes |
| GET V/search?project_id=...&q=...&mode=lexical&document_kind=...&document_kind=...&limit=...&cursor=... | 200 SearchPage, including empty hits; 409 not-ready/generation mismatch; 410 expired continuation; 422 input/unsupported capability; 503 store/config unavailable; 504 whole-operation deadline. M2 primitive, M3 later composes |
| POST V/search/rebuild?project_id=... with {idempotency_key} | Phase baseline intent only; **blocked on approved M1 completed-work/new-run interface**. OWNER/ADMIN delegated role, audit/idempotency/admission required. No 202 until durable M1 intent exists. Eligible FAILED-work retry already uses existing delivery retry route/RetryRequest, not a fabricated rebuild method |

Error envelope remains {error:{code,message,details}} with safe codes e.g. search_not_ready, search_generation_changed, search_cursor_expired, search_integrity_failed, search_capacity_exceeded, search_backend_unavailable, search_deadline_exceeded. Distinguish unavailable semantic capability from empty hits; no fallback labeling. M6 freezes exact transport mapping later, preserving repeated filters/statuses. M2 creates no Express routes or ContextBundle. C1 consumer fixtures are supplied to M3/M6 without starting their implementation.

### 7.9 Exact proposed file/action inventory

All NEW paths below are proposals, not discovered files; package markers are included where required. No deletion is proposed.

| Path / action | Components, responsibility and dependencies | Verification / rationale |
|---|---|---|
| NEW backend/platform/knowledge/__init__.py, model.py | C1 version, KnowledgeRecord/KnowledgeProjection/SearchManifest/SearchVerificationResult; immutable typed validation | Golden schemas/hash vectors/unsupported versions; cohesive M2 domain |
| NEW backend/platform/knowledge/contracts.py | SearchRequest/Hit/Page/Status/ResolvedSearchGeneration, bounded profiles, reader/verifier/writer ports and safe exceptions | M3/M6 JSON contract fixtures; no duplicate C0 types |

| NEW backend/platform/knowledge/elasticsearch.py | SearchIndexWriter and literal scoped query/readback adapter; pinned mappings/settings validation, bulk/PIT/cancellation, restricted credentials | Actual ES partial bulk/mapping/timeout/UUID/scope tests; no raw DSL port |

| NEW backend/platform/knowledge/persistence.py, repository.py | M2 resource/generation/read-pin schema and bounded SQL; enlisted SearchPublicationPort, exact accepted reads and cleanup exclusion | Real PG rollback/foreign scope/late publication/cleanup races; no M1 attempt table access |
| NEW backend/platform/knowledge/service.py | SearchWorkHandler, SearchProjectionService/SearchReader/SearchMaintenance; compose source/projector/ES/metadata, freshness/cursor/pin orchestration | C0 fixture conformance and deadline/recovery tests; no new background orchestrator |

M1 lifecycle/SQL/runtime untouched; no producer installed until accepted |
| MODIFY backend/api/v1/router.py, backend/platform/config.py, backend/infra/migrations/env.py — additive composition | Register M2 routes/settings/metadata only. Secrets/default-free numeric profiles/cursor key rotation; M1 env/policy unchanged | Safe imports/missing config => unavailable, not backend startup crash; legacy metadata discrepancy checked |
| NEW backend/infra/migrations/versions/<allocated_revision>_create_search_projection.py | Allocate real revision against current head; only M2 tables/constraints/indexes | Isolated upgrade/downgrade/re-upgrade, no frozen table alteration |
| MODIFY requirements.txt, infra/docker/compose.yml, .env.example (and ignored local .env only if separately requested) | Explicit approved compatible ES/client pair, authenticated TLS/health/persistence/resource setup, blank deployment secrets and measured profiles | Validate root compose include/env resolution without printing secrets; no changes to existing services or fabricated password |
